## `01_Architecture/` — how it works

- [[anti-survivorship-bias]] — ⭐ **WE DO NOT GET TO FORGET THE LOSERS.** Two load-bearing ideas, both **decided as principle**, neither **built**. **(1) The denominator must include the failures, or the win rate is a lie by omission.** [[Product Identity]] §5 rests the whole whale layer on *a tracked win rate* — this is what makes that rate worth trusting. A whale's win % is honest only if it counts **every** call and its outcome, losers included; amount separates a lucky hit from edge, but **the denominator separates a real track record from a curated one**, and it comes first. ⚠️ **A live hazard, not a theoretical one:** forward-only scoring, alert decay, `Fading`/`Resolved` themes and the 7-day dedup all make signals leave view for reasons unrelated to whether they were *right*, so a rate over "what is still visible" is survivorship-biased by mechanisms that already exist and were built for good reasons. **(2) The deeper one — "the signals were real, the narrative was wrong."** A convergence can be **fully genuine** — the insider really bought on the open market, the contract really landed — and the ticker still drops 70%. That is **the OPPOSITE of RTX** (where the signals themselves were fake) and **attribution repair cannot touch it**; the two are indistinguishable from the outcome alone, so without a record of *what was believed and why*, every wrong-narrative loss gets misdiagnosed as an attribution defect and rules that were never broken get "repaired". Candidate causes to tell apart over time, **not** to guess: priced-in · a correlation that broke · convergence doesn't predict here · the thesis was flawed on its own terms. **Consequence — the convergence post-mortem / falsification layer:** every convergence should carry **(a)** the thesis at fire time, **(b)** a falsification condition (*"wrong if X"*), **(c)** an honest resolution — and ⭐ **losses get the MOST detailed record, not the least**, which inverts every product's natural gravity. **The seed already ships:** the RTX card's *"Thesis invalidated if — No falsification conditions set… add one in Notes"* — Scope already knows it should record why a thesis would be wrong; this makes that field **load-bearing** instead of advisory. **Wiring:** the whale win-rates read from this record or they are dishonest; the convergence outcome-scoring flagged OPEN in [[Product Identity]] §4 **IS** this layer (one source of truth, do not build it twice); the analyst-AI writes both thesis and post-mortem **descriptively** — it never says buy or sell. **It changes nothing about the gate or detection-time scores** — it is a record of what happened *after*. ⚠️ **OPEN:** when a convergence "resolves" (inherits the BLOCKED mode axis — do not scaffold a trigger to unblock it) · measured against what (**leaning SPY/benchmark-relative**, since a 70% drop in a 75%-down market is a different lesson from one in a flat market; `alert_outcomes` already carries `benchmark_return_*`) · falsification required or optional. ⛔ **Do NOT scaffold a half-honest version** — an outcome record that resolves the easy wins and leaves losses `pending` is **worse than none**, because it looks like a track record while being exactly the bias it was meant to remove. The Scope-shaped *pain + reflection = progress*.
- [[Product Identity]] — ⭐ **WHAT SCOPE IS, AND THE LINES IT DOES NOT CROSS.** **Decided:** a **configurable research interface** for finding anything investment-relevant, **US + Canadian tickers** for now, with **AI-as-analyst** in two roles (one summarizes like a real analyst, one connects everything and makes it presentable). **The AI surfaces and explains; it NEVER instructs** — it describes what a signal is and what it has meant historically, never "buy this." **The user may act on any single signal if they choose** (invest purely on a Pelosi buy if they like) — Scope does not forbid it; Pelosi's buy is *a signal presented with its provenance*, and Scope's job is to surface it honestly with track record and context. ⚠️ **The guardrail is adopted NOW, while there is exactly one user:** ✅ *"X bought Y, here is their track record and committee"* / ❌ *"you should buy Y"* — because **retrofitting "stop giving advice" into an advice-shaped system is painful**, and public use changes the legal/ethical shape of "here's a signal". **Audience:** today **the user only**, not marketed; **intended later for others**, which is precisely why the descriptive discipline lands now. ⚠️ **MODES (sniper = short-term/recent fires/penny stocks · overwatch = long-term/macro/data-driven) are ASPIRATIONAL and BLOCKED — lenses over the same engine, driving horizon, tracked-vs-sniped convergences, and precision/recall — sitting on top of reliable outcome tracking, honest instruments, resolved-convergence history and the provenance layer, none of which exist. Recorded as north star; DO NOT SCAFFOLD.** ✅ **But the cheap capture-now SEED is a standing Phase 3 instruction: as each rule is repaired, tag its natural time horizon** (insider = short · 13F = long/slow · contract = medium · earnings = immediate) — and **the seed is already half-planted**: `alerts.time_horizon` exists, `calculate_opportunity_score` consumes it, `/alerts?mode=` already branches on it, yet **only `rule_10_corroboration.py` sets it deliberately** — everything else inherits a default or the `m008b` backfill guess. **Convergence lifecycle:** expiry is decided (Emerging/Fading); tracking-vs-sniping is the mode axis and inherits its blocked status; ⚠️ **the PRIORITY of outcome-scoring a convergence is OPEN** — recorded as a known dependency of the whale layer, not scheduled. **Whale guardrail (load-bearing):** provenance must be **EVIDENCE-first, not a story** — a tracked win rate is evidence; *"knows a general, therefore their defense buys are informed"* is a story, and **a story mapping defense-person → defense-tickers is the basket disease with a LinkedIn photo**; **a connection may never CREATE a signal, only annotate one that already stands on tracked evidence** — provenance says *why*, the win rate says *whether*. **And the staging meta-principle:** every aspiration has a cheap capture-now seed (provenance → track win rates now; signed-signal engine → sign each rule as attribution comes good; modes → tag horizon now; globe → capture located events now) — **don't build the cathedral; lay the stone the cathedral will need, while you are already standing there.**

## `08_Ideas/` — idea inbox

- [[The OSINT Globe]] — **north star for a full OSINT rewrite: geography as the organizing principle.** OSINT is **not an alert rule** — it is a **geospatial context and early-warning layer**, a globe you zoom into where **place** organizes everything: a DA elected, a governor changed, oil discovered, a war event, pinned to its location, with economically-linked tickers **beside the place they are tied to**. Still **excluded — never a gate leg**. **Why it is the fix and not a coat of paint:** today's rule indexes on **theme** (news → category → a hardcoded basket), so LMT fires on every conflict because it *lives in the defense basket*, not because it is at the event. Indexing on **place** makes the same-tickers-on-everything bug **disappear by construction** — proximity draws the eye, not theme. **It is the clusters disease one level up:** an event identified by its *theme* instead of by the place and entities actually in it, exactly as an issuer was identified by its typed ticker. Context and early warning are **one mechanic read two ways**; it joins the **spatial-UI family** with [[The World-Web]]'s ball pit (coverage) and the relationship graph (corroboration) — form matching the truth of the data. Today's RULE_OSINT is named for what it is: a **non-spatial stub** that kept "news → tickers" and threw away the place. **Scope recorded honestly** — geolocation, ticker↔place mapping, an event source, a WebGL build; **first step is a read-only audit, not a build.** Open questions flagged, not answered.

- [[Ideas Backlog]] — the capture inbox for raw ideas before they enter the plan.
- [[the-world-web-vision]] — **The World-Web: AI as connector, not oracle.** AI that *assembles* sourced facts and maps how they connect, never one that *judges*. Calibrated numbers are the objective relevance filter; a cited context-web supplies the *why*, never the *buy*. The hard rule is **receipts on every edge** — a web built from recall is a hallucinated web that looks informational. **Sequenced after convergence works in prod.**

- [[the-whale-and-provenance-layer]] — **The whale rule and the person-provenance layer.** The through-line: **the strongest signals are not events but PEOPLE with provenance** — who the trader is and what they plausibly know (win rate, relations, military background, prior employers, political alignment, "ever met Trump/Vance"). Recorded as the intended **moat**: nobody cross-references *"this small-cap buyer is a military veteran who worked at Raytheon."* The **whale rule** = a curated/earned watchlist whose **buys** are a copy-trade signal, on **track record** (realized win % *and* amount won — a single lucky call must not mint a whale; **earned**, so it waits on outcome data) and **provenance** (the *why* behind the record). ⚠️ **North star, NOT actionable, and four questions are left deliberately OPEN**: does provenance ever **gate** or only weight (*"yes, dependent"*); **where the data comes from** (**UNRESEARCHED** — scraping/ToS/accuracy minefield; hand-built profiles the safe MVP); the curated-vs-earned threshold; and **inter-ticker cross-referencing**, flagged with the warning that **it is how baskets are born**.

## `01_Architecture/` — how it works

- [[instrument-definitions-and-tiers]] — ⭐ **WHAT EACH INSTRUMENT IS FOR — read before repairing any rule.** Records the **person-provenance through-line** (the strongest signals are PEOPLE, not events) and the **convergence-value principle**: the gate decides **IF**, **ticker-relative weighting decides HOW LOUD** — a small company winning a large contract is a louder convergence than the same on a mega-cap, though the mega-cap still carries directional value. Generalizes the direction rework's cap-weighting to **every** instrument. **Five rules REDEFINED:** **RULE_01B** from "a member traded" → "a member **whose background makes this trade plausibly informed**" (committee relevance, military/veteran background, prior employers, alignment, relations — *a veteran buying an unknown defence small-cap is genuinely valuable*); **RULE_02** from cluster size → **CROSS-PARTISAN AGREEMENT** (a Democrat *and* a Republican on the same ticker — when people who disagree on everything agree, that strips partisan-thesis noise); **RULE_16** → **dual role**, confirmation/regime for big names *and* **small-cap discovery**, where 13F's ~45-day staleness stops mattering because the move is slow (**weighting between the two: OPEN**); **RULE_09** → **demoted out of the gate** — lobbying measures influence on *government*, not tickers, and forcing it per-ticker is the basket disease; **RULE_08** → **context, door open** to a narrow instrument only under **document-names-a-company** attribution ⚠️ **PROVISIONAL pending the user's own reading**. Also records the target **Tier 1–3 + context** shape (T1 insider/whale/cap-weighted contracts · T2 person-provenance congressional · T3 earnings + 13F · context: lobbying, OSINT, fed-register) and the design rule: **a good number of instruments, each with a DISTINCT job — not many noisy duplicates of "something happened."** ✅ Links to **[[Make It Real campaign]]**, which now exists and carries the ordered repair list; note that Master Plan's "Phase 3" is a *different* phase (structural reasoning, iPhone 12) and must not be confused with the campaign's rule repairs.

## `02_Sessions/` — work log
- [[SESSION-2026-08-16-position-close-pnl]] — ✅ **CLOSE-OUT + REALIZED P&L, WITH A BENCHMARK THAT CANNOT BE OMITTED — verified, NOT merged** (`feat/position-close` off **`feat/position-ledger`** `9250593`, not off main; `3735d9b` + `be79c54`; 3 files; no rule/gate/scoring file opened). **Stage 0's four decisions:** append-only `position_closes` (never an UPDATE — the entry module's zero-UPDATE property is what makes "a pass row can never become a buy" *structural*; cost stated: `positions.status` never moves, liveness is one LEFT JOIN, citing insider_clusters' fourteen-bugs-were-one-bug lesson); keys on **position_id, never ticker** (ticker is a projection — several open positions per ticker are allowed, so `/sell AAPL` refuses and lists candidates); benchmark reconstructed from **daily closes** with the asymmetry *stored* in `benchmark_basis`, not smoothed; and **one numeric gate** — `validate_price()` extracted and called by entry, exit AND benchmark, because the +inf/bool trap appeared twice precisely *because* there were two copies. ⭐ **Ground truth PROVEN — after I threw away a first anchor that was worthless:** I'd seeded entry = the live quote, so P&L was 0.0 and proved nothing. Re-anchored at entry 450.0 → +45.40 / +10.0889% and SPY 770.56→776.34 = +0.75011%, both matching stored to the cent and basis point. ⭐ **The anchor itself exposed a real defect:** `holding_days` 5.19 sat beside a 3-day benchmark window (Sunday run, last close Friday) — now the span is stated in days, and the operator line no longer claims "over the same window", which is literally false whenever the market was shut at either end. 🔴 **THE VERIFIER FOUND FOUR, ONE ALARMING: `/sell ٢` (Arabic-Indic digit) SILENTLY CLOSED A POSITION.** `str.isdigit()` is not a number check and failed both ways — `"²"` is isdigit-True but `int()` raises (retried HTTP 500), while `"٢"` is isdigit-True **and** `int("٢")==2`, resolving to a real id. `isdecimal()` would NOT have fixed it (also True for `٢`); only the ASCII check does. 🔴 **And the benchmark could truncate silently while the row blamed a holiday:** a corrupt SPY bar was dropped without a trace, shortening the window (**1.7964% → 0.3992%, a 1.40pp error** into the human-facing "vs benchmark" line) while the basis asserted the market was shut — on a day it was open. *A wrong cause beside a wrong number is worse than the number alone: it tells the reader not to look.* Also: `replied: True` was unconditional (close recorded, nobody told); and my own structural surface-test globbed only 2 subtrees while its name promised the whole tree. All four fixed and re-attacked. **Verifier upheld:** one P&L surface only (whole-tree grep, all file types), `realized_pnl` bit-for-bit from the two stored prices with no re-fetched entry, 16-thread barrier concurrency → exactly 1 close row. `format_pnl()` takes benchmark and n as **required keyword args**, so omitting either is a TypeError. Suite **1627 passed**. ⚠️ **n of closed positions = ZERO** — neither table exists in prod; every number here is seeded test data and no performance conclusion is available or will be for a long time.
- [[SESSION-2026-08-16-position-ledger]] — ✅ **A LEDGER WHOSE ENTRY PRICE IS REAL OR ABSENT — built, adversarially verified, NOT merged** (`feat/position-ledger` off `main` `2b2cfe7`; `97bbd37` + `9250593`; 4 files; isolated worktree, shared tree never touched; **no rule/gate/scoring/corroboration file opened**, `jpt_common` untouched). Telegram Buy/Pass buttons → a `positions` row with a live quote fetched at tap time. ⭐ **The reuse the work order asked for would have been wrong, and reading it caught that:** `position_sizing.py` reads the Yahoo *daily close series*, so a Tuesday 14:00 tap would have recorded **Monday's close under a Tuesday timestamp** — back-dating the entry by a day and a price move, the exact poisoning the contract forbids arriving *through* the requested reuse. Same host/endpoint/`_YF_HEADERS`, but reads `meta.regularMarketPrice` (also the exact-to-the-cent one: 305.93 vs the series' 305.92999267578125), storing `quote_market_time` separately from `quote_fetched_at` so weekend staleness stays visible. **Ground truth PROVEN:** hand-checked MSFT 495.4 → ledger recorded 495.4 and the trade time exactly. ⚠️ Run on a Sunday, so the "live" quote is correctly Friday's close — a real last trade, but not a price anyone could fill at, which is precisely why the two timestamps are separate columns. 🔴 **THE VERIFIER BROKE IT TWICE, both in the guards written to stop those very things.** **(B1)** `not (price > 0)` caught NaN (`nan > 0` is False) but admitted **`+inf`** — and it needs no exotic token, `json.loads("1e999")` yields `inf` from ordinary valid JSON. I'd found this much myself but had it as an edge case; **it is not** — the row was **COMMITTED**, the operator told *"Recorded at inf"*, and only *then* did the response 500, so the poison **survived behind the 500** and Telegram's retry answered `duplicate`. Fixed with `math.isfinite` + an upper bound; `CLAUDE.md` records this same non-finite-JSON trap in `leg_weights` — **second appearance**. Same path: `bool` is an `int` subclass, so `"regularMarketPrice": true` was becoming a $1.00 entry. **(A2)** `hmac.compare_digest` **raises** on a non-ASCII `str` and ASGI decodes headers latin-1, so one high byte turned the 403 into an **unlogged 500**, breaking "every rejected write leaves a trace" on the app's only inbound write endpoint. Both attacks re-run against the fix, not assumed closed. ⭐ **Headline UPHELD:** after line-by-line hunting for SQL defaults, module state, mutable default args and cross-request leakage, **no path exists that writes an `entry_price` not from a live tap-time fetch**; idempotency proven by *genuine concurrency* (12 threads × 4 trials, each returning a different price → 1 row, 11 clean `duplicate`s with the winner's real id). **Judgment calls:** no buttons on **basket** alerts — the codebase resolves `"LMT RTX NOC"` to its first symbol, right for a display badge and wrong for a decision that would silently commit you to LMT for a three-company signal; and a `pass` is recorded even when the quote fails while a `buy` is not (on a decline the decision is the datum, on a buy the price is). **Worthiness recorded NULL** — no `worthiness_scores` table exists in prod (57 tables, verified) and the module refuses to recompute one inline; stated coverage gap, not papered over. Suite **1597 passed**. ⚠️ Needs `TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET` set + `setWebhook` pointed at the handler before it does anything; buttons deliberately don't render until then.
- [[SESSION-2026-08-16-worthiness-score-fixes]] — 🔴 **STILL NOT READY FOR SIGN-OFF — the two requested fixes are correctly implemented, verified to the digit, and neither one gives the composite real predictive power; one of them makes its own validation metric worse.** Fixed exactly the two defects the prior session's verifier found (Signal Quality and build-now-vs-later left explicitly open, per the mission). **Freshness fix:** excludes `lifecycle_stage='retracted'` alerts entirely and computes relative to the outcome window's close (anchor + 20 days, no look-ahead) instead of "today" — verified GNPX/MAN/CBZ now correctly score `FR=0` (their only-ever alert is retracted). **Instrument Breadth fix:** now calls the gate's real `alert_corroborates()` so a RULE_06 leg only counts with `corroborates=1` — verified digit-for-digit against the prior verifier's corrected spec (GE 3→2 instruments, CDNA/EDBL/RUBI/INOD/AMWL 1→0). **Re-ran the identical 17-ticker sample: r=0.2051 — but an independent verifier's permutation test (200k label shuffles) shows this sits almost exactly on the null distribution's own mean (p≈0.43, 95% CI [-0.31,+0.62], wide enough to contain every prior session's r at once), and on the FULL 3,677-outcome prod population it collapses to r=0.0005.** Worse: isolated, the Freshness fix alone gives r=0.0343 — *below* the uncorrected 0.0486 baseline — making it a net negative on the composite's own stated metric. Also self-corrected a wrong Spearman figure (0.0844, a rounding/tie artifact — real value ≈0.05) rather than let it stand. **Two problems surfaced beyond this session's scope, flagged not fixed:** retraction is now honored in Freshness but still ignored in Instrument Breadth (GNPX/MAN/CBZ still bank breadth points off the same retracted alert that zeroes their freshness); and 98% of all retracted alerts share one single bulk-retraction timestamp, so "exclude retracted" is functionally "was this ticker touched by the July-8 batch" — and that zeroed group's real alpha was actually slightly *better* than the non-zeroed group's. Verdict: the deeper problem isn't a bugs problem — whether to build this now vs. after Scope's real convergence data has grown is the actual decision in front of Maksim.
- [[SESSION-2026-08-16-worthiness-score-design]] — 🔴 **DESIGN PROPOSAL, NOT READY FOR SIGN-OFF — checked the composite against real prod data and it doesn't discriminate (r=0.05), plus a verifier caught a real bug and a real design flaw.** Additive-only, read-only design exercise (Stage 0 recon + Stage 1 proposal per the work order's own hard stop before any implementation) for a new "worthiness" score combining `evidence_confidence`, instrument breadth, freshness, sector momentum and liquidity. **Stage 0, checked not assumed:** `evidence_confidence` is degenerate (99.6% of all 3,519 gate-eligible prod alerts sit at exactly 20.0; real max anywhere in prod is 60.0, never the documented 81 ceiling); `position_sizing_cache` (liquidity) has 3 rows in all of prod; `classify_sector` returned "Other" for 16/17 sample tickers; insider-cluster size has **no persisted table at all** and was dropped from the proposal rather than faked. **A self-caught correction:** the first Instrument Breadth draft used raw alert count, which would have scored NVDA highest for firing the same congressional-trade instrument 13 times on a popular mega-cap — fixed by switching to the gate's own `rule10_instruments()` mapper, read-only. **Sanity-checked against 17 real tickers' real `alert_outcomes` (return_20d/alpha vs SPY):** Pearson r between total score and 20d alpha = **+0.05** — essentially zero. Adversarial cases surfaced deliberately, not hidden: GE scored highest (56.11) with the weakest alpha among top scorers (+2.1%); UTZ scored only 39.24 (tied with 6 others) despite the single best outcome in the sample (+81.4% alpha); a 7-way tie at 39.24 spans -65.9% to +81.4% alpha. 🔴 **Independent verifier OVERTURNED the Instrument Breadth numbers** — missed the gate's *second*, per-alert signed-leg check (`SIGNED_RULES={RULE_06}`, `corroborates` NULL/0 fails closed), so GE's real instrument count is 2 not 3 (loses its top rank once corrected; corrected r rises only to 0.25, still not significant at n=17). **And found a real design flaw this session missed: Freshness pays full credit for `lifecycle_stage='retracted'` alerts** — GNPX/MAN/CBZ's top-scoring "freshest signal" is literally a withdrawn alert. Also confirmed a 1,166-alert/1,166-ticker/2-second backfill batch (predates all `activity_log` rows) is contaminating the outcome sample. **Explicit recommendation to fix the retraction bug and the signed-leg omission before this goes back for sign-off** — Stage 2 (the scoring table + scheduled job) not started, per the work order's own hard stop.
- [[SESSION-2026-08-16-drawer-merge-and-filing-velocity]] — ✅ **PART 1 MERGED** (`main` = **`2b2cfe7`**, local, **NOT pushed**) + 🔴 **PART 2 NO-GO at the Stage 0 gate.** **PART 1:** `fix/alert-confidence-drawer-honesty` merged. Both named surprises were present — **`main` had moved** (`f789b44`→`e838453`, two merges) and **another session is live** in the shared tree with 17 uncommitted files — but the surprise was **resolved by investigation, not ignored**: the conflict surface is **EMPTY** (my 5 files vs the 15 main gained, zero intersection), so no conflict was structurally possible. **The shared tree was never touched**; the merge ran in an isolated worktree. Suite 1547 pre-merge → **1567 on merged main**, same 2 pre-existing failures. So the guarded resolver was **REUSED, not reimplemented** — `_market_cap_core` carries five plausibility guards this repo paid for one at a time, and it was handed this module's own cache accessors with `shared_write=None`. ⭐ **The isolation property is the whole design:** `contract_leg_weight` weights a contracts leg **only when a live `ticker_meta` row already exists**, so a panel that warmed it would let a *page view* change `evidence_confidence`. Proven by a **whole-DB 35-table digest** across a real resolve with a **pre-seeded stale `ticker_meta` row left untouched**. **Anchor:** BA alert 28182 — `$31,972,918,249.03` is **17.4615%** of a `$183,105,021,086` cap and **34.0156%** of `$93,995,000,000` TTM revenue, re-derived from raw SEC+Yahoo by **two independent routes**. 🔴 **Six bugs found during the build, two of them mine and serious:** BA's "TTM revenue" resolved to a **2019** figure (the 606 tag holds 3 facts ending 2019-12-31 while `Revenues` is current, and the code returned on the first concept with rows); and a comment containing **backticks inside a template literal** closed it early — **the entire ticker page was dead** while the Python suite stayed green, now guarded by a `node --check` test that was itself checked against the reintroduced defect. Also: a fabricated `/feed?alert=<id>` deep link (no such param), an inclusive-date burn-rate error, a cap TTL mismatch serving month-old caps stamped fresh, and a leaked SQLite connection. ⭐ **Dilution overhang is `unavailable`, and that is a FINDING, not a TODO** — Scope ingests no SEC filing text, ATM capacity is prose-only, and `ClassOfWarrantOrRightOutstanding` is dimensioned per class (ONDS reads 267,857, down from 3,616,071, and cannot be shown to be complete). There is deliberately **no dilution column**. **Verifier overturned the delivery headline and found 4 real defects, all fixed:** a reported **`$0` relabelled "unavailable" with a factually false reason** (server used truthiness, client used `status`); **52/53-week filers falling back to a stale `FY` figure — 10 of 34 sampled tickers** (QCOM, AMD, INTC, MU, LMT, GD…), fixed with a ±7-day window on the prior-year *end* plus a span check on the telescoped result; `last_close` undated and market cap's **resolve timestamp mislabelled `as_of`**; and the events header hardcoding **"% of TTM rev"** over an `FY` denominator.
- [[SESSION-2026-08-15-security-audit-fixes]] — 🔒 **ALL FIVE SECURITY FINDINGS FIXED AND VERIFIED AGAINST A REAL RUNNING SERVER, nothing merged** (`security/fix-audit-findings` off `main` `c9d86ce`; 7 files incl. new `api/rate_limit.py`; human-gated). **(1) Stored XSS** — fixed the audit's finding AND a worse one found while fixing: `watchlist.html`'s Remove button built `onclick="removeTicker('${w.symbol}')"` by string interpolation, where HTML-entity-escaping the quote does **not** actually help (the browser decodes entities in attribute values *before* compiling the result as JS) — rewritten to a `data-symbol` attribute + delegated listener instead. Also found `POST /tickers/watchlist/{symbol}` had **zero server-side validation** (client-side filter only), a lower-barrier XSS than the one the audit flagged — closed with a server-side ticker-shape regex. **(2)** `/admin/upload-db` now requires the real SQLite magic header + a `PRAGMA integrity_check` on a temp copy before the swap, a 500MB ceiling, and `hmac.compare_digest` instead of a timing-leaky `!=`; proven live that a truncated-but-valid-header file gets rejected **without touching the live DB** (integrity_check still "ok" afterward). **(3)** `/chat`'s system prompt now frames the user's message as data, not instructions, wrapped in `<user_message>` tags, plus a 2000-char cap that didn't exist before. **(4)** New in-memory per-IP rate limiter on `/chat`, both admin routes, and both watchlist write endpoints — proven live, 429s fire at exactly the configured thresholds. **(5)** CORS `allow_credentials` flipped to `False` (Scope sets zero cookies, so nothing was being protected by `True`, and it's a known-bad pairing with `allow_origins=["*"]` regardless). ⚠️ Found and left unfixed as an unrelated bug: `/admin/refresh` throws `NameError: LIVE_RULES` on the happy path — confirmed pre-existing on clean `main`. Ran concurrently with another session's uncommitted `position-sizing` work in the same shared directory; the two overlapping files (`tickers.py`, `ticker.html`) were committed via hand-isolated `git apply --cached` hunks so only this session's changes landed, leaving the other session's uncommitted work undisturbed. Full suite **1539 passed**, same 3 pre-existing failures.
- [[SESSION-2026-08-15-security-audit]] — 🔴 **READ-ONLY SECURITY AUDIT: unauthenticated stored XSS and an unvalidated admin DB-replace endpoint, both real.** Confirmed architecture first: no accounts/sessions/cookies/uploads/payments (repo-wide grep, zero hits); Groq/`llama-3.3-70b` narrative generation is real and live (`chat.py` + 6 more files). **Top finding:** `POST /api/watchlist-rules` needs zero auth, and `watchlist.html:229-240`'s `renderRules()` interpolates `label`/`condition_type`/`condition_value` into `innerHTML` with **no escaping at all** — a stored XSS anyone can plant with a plain POST, no secret required. **Second:** `POST /admin/upload-db` (`main.py:460-481`) accepts **any** bytes ≥4KB and `shutil.move`s them straight over the live DB — no file-type/SQLite-header check, no upper size bound, gated by a plain `!=` (non-constant-time) compare against `ADMIN_KEY` passed in the URL query string (so it lands in logs). **Third, as flagged going in:** prompt injection on `/chat` is genuinely undefended — `req.message` goes straight into the Groq prompt with no isolation, though blast radius is bounded (no tool-calling, no other users' private data to leak). **Fourth:** zero rate-limiting anywhere in the codebase, including the Groq-backed `/chat` endpoint — unbounded real-money cost exposure. Also: CORS is `allow_origins=["*"]` **+** `allow_credentials=True` together (a known-bad pairing, low practical impact today only because no cookies exist to ride on it). 8 of 20 items N/A with real architecture reasons stated, not padded.
- [[SESSION-2026-08-15-ui-audit-fixes]] — 🔧 **ALL FIVE UI-AUDIT ITEMS FIXED AND BROWSER-VERIFIED, nothing merged** (`fix/ui-audit-batch` off `main` `ef227f2`; 6 files; human-gated). **(1)** The 7-vs-14-days dishonest copy — confirmed `CONVERGENCE_WINDOW_DAYS=14` is still the live gate window, found the SAME bug on the `converge` branch too (not just the "quiet" one the audit cited), fixed by threading a real `window_days` value through every hero mode instead of a second hardcoded number. **(2)** Watchlist delete now needs a real `confirm()` that genuinely blocks the DELETE — proven with network-level evidence: cancel path fires **zero** DELETE requests, confirm path fires exactly one, both re-verified after a full page reload. **(3)** Copy-to-clipboard shipped on `/feed` and `/ticker/*` — **not** `/signals`, which turned out to be pure canvas with no discrete alert DOM element to attach a button to (audit's assumption corrected on inspection, not silently forced). **(4)** Skip-to-content added once in `nav.js` for all ~31 pages — caught and fixed a real bug where the fallback target-picker landed on a `visibility:hidden` legend div on `/universe`, making `.focus()` a silent no-op; fixed by checking real visibility AND re-resolving the target fresh on every activation. **(5)** Sticky headers required real CSS-spec debugging on `/history`: `overflow-x:auto` alone was silently forcing `overflow-y` to `auto` too (per spec), trapping sticky inside a wrapper that never itself scrolled; fixed by embracing a bounded 70vh internally-scrolling pane instead of fighting it — which then surfaced a SECOND bug, `border-collapse:collapse` breaking the sticky cell's own paint box (scrolled text visibly bled through the opaque header), fixed via `border-collapse:separate`. Full suite **1520 passed**; the 3 failures independently confirmed pre-existing on clean `main` via `git stash`, not introduced here.

Also fixed the **null-overwrite trap** the fix would otherwise have created (`upsert_filing` wrote `member_id` unconditionally → `COALESCE`), and added `check_term_mismatches` — ⭐ `unmatched_filers` records names the matcher **failed** on and was **EMPTY** while 22 filings sat on dead members, so a guard keyed on failure cannot see a confident wrong answer. Guard **tested by making it FIRE**, not by observing silence. Suite 1,562 pass / 3 pre-existing (confirmed on a `git stash`ed clean tree).

The signing session's own guard was, in the verifier's phrase, *"a docstring with a tautology attached"*: it asserted synthetic dicts don't corroborate, which holds whether or not the population path ever ran. ⭐ **THE PRECISION POINT AND THE WHOLE DESIGN: it keys on `corroborates IS NULL` ("no verdict on record"), NEVER on the corroboration boolean.** A signed rule whose in-window candidates are all SALES has every verdict `0` and is **healthy** — keying on the boolean would make the alarm fire loudest exactly when signing is doing its job. - [[SESSION-2026-08-01-novelty-like-substring]] — ✅ **THE NOVELTY ANCHOR IS A TOKEN, NOT A SUBSTRING — the backlog's first cross-cutting fix; the two obvious versions of it were both wrong, and so were two of my own claims** (`fix/novelty-like-substring` off `main` `ce1c7be`; **not merged, not deployed, not committed**; 1176 passed / 5 xfailed / **0 failed** under `TZ=UTC`; working DB md5 unchanged). `calculate_novelty_score` matched `LIKE '%anchor%'` — a **substring** match, and SQLite's LIKE is case-**insensitive**. Every RULE_02 headline reads `"Cluster: N members … TICKER …"` and **`Cl·us·ter` contains "us"**, so `LIKE '%US%'` matched **82 of 82** RULE_02 rows; `T` and `ST` likewise. ⛔ **The obvious fix — match the `ticker` column — is UNSAFE:** it moves **53.8% of rows**, is unreachable for the **450 NULL-ticker** and **511 composite** rows, and **destroys RULE_CLUSTER's novelty**, which passes `novelty_key=<fingerprint>` and *deliberately embeds it in `why_matters`* so novelty counts prior occurrences of **that cluster**, not ticker mentions (`rule_cluster.py:229,270`). ⛔ **And boundary-matching EVERYTHING regresses 450 rows:** the `headline[:30]` fallback is cut **mid-word**, so a trailing boundary can never match it and those rows strand at novelty **1.0 — the maximum**, trading a known bug for a bigger one. ✅ **The fix is gated on the ANCHOR'S OWN SHAPE** (`len ≤ 5`, alpha-initial, `[A-Z0-9.]`), so it is **closed by structure, not by a rule list** — a rule written tomorrow passing a short ticker is covered the day it is written. Padded **GLOB** (`REGEXP` is registered nowhere; `create_function` has zero hits) collapses prefix/suffix/whole-string into one pattern and needs **no escaping *because of* the gate** (a ticker-shaped anchor cannot contain `* ? [ ]`). Every non-ticker anchor keeps the original query verbatim. ⚠️ **CORRECTED — my first real-data numbers were measured with the 30-day window REMOVED.** "`US` 82→4, novelty 0.185→0.383" is an **all-time** figure the windowed function does not produce; the truth is `US` **6→0, 0.339→1.0** (there are genuinely zero in-window RULE_02 `US` alerts, so 1.0 is right and 0.339 was the corrupted value). And the blast radius is far larger than the two anchors I showed: **129 of 1345 in-window `(rule, anchor)` pairs move**, every move *reduces* the count, so the change is **monotone — novelty can only RISE**. The worst distortion is in **RULE_ANOMALY, a rule I never mentioned**: `S` matched **1023 of 1023** of its alerts and sat at **0.126, barely above the 0.1 floor** → 1 row, **0.591** (`T` 1023→2, `A` 1023→4, `W` 881→2, `EA` 873→1). ⚠️ **A REGRESSION I INTRODUCED AND THE VERIFIER CAUGHT: GLOB is case-sensitive, which I first wrote up as a feature.** `rule_anomaly.py:112` interpolates the **raw** ticker (`ecor`) into the headline while `alerts.ticker` is uppercased, so **6 real rows stopped matching themselves and jumped to novelty 1.0 — the ceiling**, over-scoring in the *same direction* as the original bug. Fixed by `upper()`ing both columns; the boundary alone still kills the `Cl·US·ter` match. Pinned by a test. ⚠️ **And I cited a test that passes vacuously:** `test_phase2_intel.py::test_scores_are_not_all_defaults` early-returns on an empty isolated DB (`:143`), so it is **zero evidence** for this change. Pre-existing; flagged, not fixed. ⚠️ **Closed a pre-existing gap:** RULE_CLUSTER's `novelty_key`→`why_matters` coupling **had no test at all** — you could delete that leg and the whole suite stayed green. Now pinned, with a discriminator proving fingerprint novelty is per-**cluster** not per-ticker. **Mutation-tested — round 1 caught 3, then a verifier found 5 SURVIVORS** (`len≤5`→`≤8`; the 30-day window and the `rule=?` scoping inside the GLOB branch; digit boundaries; the trailing boundary). **All 9 mutations now caught** by `tests/test_novelty_anchor_boundary.py` (**43 cases**). Formula, floor, window and return tail verified **md5-identical to `main`**; **no `UPDATE`, no migration** — forward-only, detection-time scores immutable. ❌ **RETRACTED 2026-08-01 — the "511 composite rows sit at novelty 1.0" flag was WRONG (mine, inferred, never measured):** **zero of the 511 are at 1.0** (range 0.155–0.591, mode 0.155) — at detection time the anchor was the `$`-prefixed raw string, which RULE_07 embeds verbatim in its own headline, so each basket self-matched. See [[SESSION-2026-08-01-novelty-composite-ticker]] — **NO-GO**. Also unfixed: `CTA.PB` (6 chars) falls outside the `len ≤ 5` gate and keeps the old branch (1 row). **Residual stated honestly:** a standalone token in unrelated prose (`US` in "US Steel") still counts, and after the case fix a lowercase *word* spelling a ticker counts too — closing that needs attribution, not string matching.

- [[SESSION-2026-07-30-anti-survivorship-bias-principle]] — 📝 **BACKFILLED 2026-08-01, and the lateness is the point.** Documentation-only session that recorded [[anti-survivorship-bias]] — *we do not get to forget the losers* — as a first-class principle: **(1) the denominator must include the failures or the win rate is a lie by omission** (this is what makes the tracked win rate [[Product Identity]] §5 rests the whole whale layer on actually worth trusting), and **(2) "the signals were real, the narrative was wrong"** — a convergence can be fully genuine and still lose, which is the **opposite** of RTX and which **attribution repair cannot touch**. Plus the layer that requires: thesis-at-fire · falsification condition · honest resolution, with **losses getting the MOST detailed record**. **Beyond the brief:** the survivorship hazard is **live here, not theoretical** — forward-only scoring, decay, `Fading` and the dedup window already make signals leave view for reasons unrelated to whether they were *right*, so nobody would have to do anything wrong for the bias to appear. Cross-linked into `signed-signal-engine` (signing fixes attribution, **not** narrative), the whale note, and `Product Identity` §4. ⚠️ `[[Make It Real campaign]]` is referenced by three notes but **has never been written** — linked *to*, not invented. ❌ **This session originally left NO log** — the vault *document* was mistaken for the record. A deliverable is not a log; memory updated so it cannot recur.

`(ticker,amount)`, 10/10 verified against source) — ⚠️ **but `ingested_at` is UNSOUND as a boundary for contract rows**: `upsert_award` never rewrites it, so `contracts id 296` (LMT $4,761,000,000,
